| 611 | A style rule `insertRule()` adds goes into the rule set the document's resolver has instead of a new rule set of all sheets (adds to #530) | Patch 0158. `Style::Scope::didChangeStyleSheetRule` keeps the resolver while only style rules or `@media` rules of style rules were inserted (it records the sheet of each); at the update `insertChangedRulesIntoResolver` gives each one an anchor, the next rule of its sheet the rule set has (`@font-face`, `@keyframes` and similar rules without rule data are skipped), else the previous one, and `RuleSet::insertRules` builds the rule data of each inserted rule in a small rule set of its own (`RuleSetBuilder::addInsertedRule`, dynamic media queries), moves every position at or after an insertion point up by the number of inserted rule data (also in the side vectors of cascade layers, container queries and `@scope` and in the dynamic media query rules), and appends the inserted data with their positions to the same buckets; `ScopeRuleSets::insertAuthorRules` adds their features and drops the feature caches. Anything else (a removed rule, another kind of rule, an anchor without rule data, a sheet with `@import` or a `media` attribute, a shadow tree, a resolver cleared meanwhile) builds the resolver anew as before; invalidation stays that of #530. | 612 | Tabs can be pinned like in Chrome, the pinned part of the strip is a section of `tabs.ts` | `Tab` gets a pinned state (`pinned`, class `.pinned`), the tab menu Pin or Unpin between Duplicate and Mute tab (`setPinned`). The order invariant lives in one place of `tabs.ts`: pinned tabs come first, and `insert` puts every tab within its `section` (a pinned tab among the pinned ones, any other right of them), used by `add` (new, link, popup, restored, reopened tabs) and `moveTab` (drag, Ctrl+Shift+PageUp/PageDown); `tabsBeside` limits the drag of `tab-drag.ts` to the section. Duplicate and Reopen closed tab keep the pin, a discarded tab too. The session entry has `pinned`; `sessionToRestore(whole)` returns all tabs or, without Restore tabs at start or with a start address (`?url=`), only the pinned ones, which open before the start page like Chrome's pinned tabs at startup. A pinned tab is as wide as its content (`container-type: normal`, so the narrow tab queries do not apply), only the icon, the speaker instead of it while sound plays (Chrome shows the alert indicator first). Found while testing: a restored tab whose first page had not committed when the demo was left (Playwright reload, a quick reload by the user) got a navigation event with an empty address at `pagehide` and fell out of the session; the tab now keeps what it loads (`restoring`) until its document commits, and the session uses `sessionURL` and `sessionHistory`. Tests: `tests/demo/pinned-tabs-test.mjs` (Chromium and Firefox; the menu has no Pin before, and the session check failed with the active restored tab still loading before `restoring`), `tests/demo/tab-menu-test.mjs` | Finding of a UI review: the tab menu had no Pin and the demo no pinned state, so a mail or calendar tab could not be kept small at the left, Close other tabs closed it and nothing kept it across starts. The border lives in `insert` rather than at every caller, so link tabs, popups, restored and reopened tabs cannot land among the pinned ones by an index of their own. Pinned tabs restored without the setting as in Chrome, where pinned tabs survive "Open the New Tab page" at startup. Not done: tab groups, a pinned marker in the phone overview |
| 614 | Image load events go out in passes of at most 16 ms and hold back their document's load event, like tasks in Blink | Patch 0159. WebKit's `EventSender` (one per kind, shared by all pages) dispatched every waiting load and error event in one timer task. A pass that its timer runs now ends like a pass of WebCore's timers (`ThreadTimers::maxDurationOfFiringTimers`, 16 ms) or when input waits (`bl_wtf_input_waiting`), after at least one event; the rest goes first in the next pass, so due timers, input and frames come between them as between tasks (the spec queues each as a task of its own, Blink posts each). The sender of images (`ImageLoader`) also delays the load event of the image's document from the moment an event waits until it is dispatched or canceled (`EventSenderLoadEventDelay`, a weak pointer to the document, so a document that goes away is not kept), like Blink's `IncrementLoadEventDelayCount` in the task; before, `Document::implicitClose` dispatched all events of the page that were still waiting in its own task right before the window's load event. That flush stays for the other kinds and other documents of the page. Test: `tests/engine/pages/image-load-events.html` (a timer started in the first of 30 listeners of 20 ms runs after 1 instead of 30 of them, all events in order, a timer started in the first listener of the document's images runs before the window's load event, which comes after all of them; the old engine fails both) | The report (Moto G71, 20min.ch): Back showed the article for 18 s and then the list at its top. On Back the site renders the list in the `popstate` task, then every image of the list fires its load event and Swiper's listener lays the page out (about 15000 `getComputedStyle` calls, Chromium as many); the one task of these events took 12 to 13 s locally (21.5 s profiled), the list painted only after it, and the site's own scroll restore (a timer 100 ms after its route change that scrolls to where it was at the tap) ran only after it. Locally (phone emulation, 3 interleaved runs each) the list is back at its position 8.6 to 9.0 s after Back instead of 20.0 to 21.0 s, the longest step after Back 4.7 to 5.1 s instead of 12.3 to 12.6 s (that is the `popstate` render of React), the tap to the article and the load unchanged (Chromium restores after about 4 s). A profiled load had the same flush in `implicitClose`, 7.3 s in one task. Not taken: making each event a task of the page's event loop (the window's load event in WebKit is no task, so the order to it would need the same delay, and the flush of the other kinds stays) |
| 615 | A page from the back/forward cache reports its icon with the restore, and a tab shows the remembered icon of a page seen before until the engine reports one | `WebView::didRestoreFromCache` (`engine/src/WebView.cpp`) calls `updateFavicon` like a change of the icon links: the restored document has all its links, its icon is usually among the 4 the view keeps and goes to the host at once, and the commit had cleared the reported address. In the demo, the navigation after a commit of another address shows `rememberedPageIcon()` of `favicons.ts` (the page's own remembered icon, not the host's latest) until the `icon` event replaces it; without one the last icon stays within the site as before; not on an error page and not for the same address (a reload). Tests: `tests/core/favicon-test.mjs` (back to a cached page that starts a frame that never loads at `pageshow`: no icon before, Chromium and Firefox), `tests/demo/favicons-test.mjs` (a page seen before whose head does not arrive shows its red icon, not the blue one of the page before on the host; failed before, Chromium and Firefox) | Finding: after back from a BBC Sport article to bbc.com/news on a Pixel the tab and its overview card kept the yellow Sport icon for minutes. The news page was cached while ads still loaded and came back without its load ending again, and the engine reported icons only from the parsed head, a link change or the load event, none of which came; the demo keeps the last icon within the site. Locally with BBC: yellow for the 60 s observed before, black from the first check after back now. Chrome looks up the favicon database for the new page at commit. Rejected: reporting the icon at every commit (a request per page for the same icon, #389), the host's latest icon at commit (that is the icon of the page left on one host) |
| 616 | A panel opened from the menu or another panel starts at its top | The menu and every panel are one scroll container, `#side-panel` (`overflow-y: auto`, on phones the bottom sheet). `togglePanel` (`apps/demo/src/side-panel.ts`) closed a panel by hiding it, which reset its scroll, but going from the menu straight to History kept the panel shown and `render()` only swapped its content, so History kept the menu's `scrollTop`: on an iPhone with the menu sheet scrolled to reach Settings, the History sheet opened with its title and close button above the sheet's top edge, and the desktop dropdown did the same. `togglePanel` now sets `scrollTop` to 0 after it rendered a newly opened panel; `refresh()` still keeps the position while the same list renders again (#544) | One scroll container for all panels stays (one sheet, one backdrop, one close watcher); resetting where a panel is opened is the single place that matches a panel opened anew, as in Chrome, whose menu and side panels each start at their top. | 617 | The iOS keyboard's field arrows open the picker of the field they move to (adds to #423) | New export `bl_show_focused_picker` (abiVersion 43): `pressFieldArrow` (`packages/core/src/keyboard.ts`) calls it in the key queue right after the Tab or Shift+Tab of an arrow, and `showPickerOfFocusedField` (`engine/src/Choosers.cpp`) opens the picker of the focused field as the browser, not the page: a date or time input without an open picker (its part in the shadow tree counts as the field), a color input, the list of a menu `<select>`; nothing for other, disabled or read only fields, nothing when the page kept the focus on a text field. It goes through `InputType::showPicker` and `HTMLSelectElement::openPickerForUserInteraction`, so it needs no user activation of the page and uses none up. A tap on a part of a date field (`WebView::touchEvent`) uses the same function for date and time fields only, which no longer uses up the page's activation either. On iPhone and iPad the request reaches the ui's tap sheet (`.bl-picker-sheet`, "Tap the field to choose") or select list like after a tap. A Tab of a real keyboard opens nothing. Test: `tests/core/safari-test.mjs` (previous to a date field and next to a select request their pickers, Shift+Tab of a keyboard to the date field none; the first two failed on the engine before) | Finding of 2026-11-18 (iPhone 16, iOS 18.6): the previous arrow from a name field focused the year of the date field above, the keyboard closed (no text input there) and nothing let the user choose a date until a tap on the field. Safari shows the date picker or the select wheel in place of the keyboard when its arrows reach such a field (WebKit's assisted elements on iOS), and WebKit opens a date chooser only on activation (a click), not on focus. The core cannot tell what the Tab focused, and opening a picker on every keyboard focus would break Tab through forms on desktops, so the arrow asks for it explicitly. Not taken: a Space key after the Tab (the page would see a key the user did not press), page script calling `showPicker()` (needs and uses up the page's activation, throws in cross-origin frames), the engine reporting the focused field's kind so the core decides (one more event for the same result) |
| 618 | Above the memory budget memory is short only while the malloc area stays full also after the release that drops the JS code and the page left last (changes #497) | `engine/src/MemoryWatch.cpp`: when a release that keeps the code and the page left last leaves less than 128 MB free inside the malloc area above `BL_MEMORY_PRESSURE_MB`, the second release drops them as before, and then `mallinfo()` is read again: `memory-pressure` is `critical` only if less than 128 MB is still free, else `moderate`. Before, the level came from the measurement before the second release. Test: `tests/core/back-cache-test.mjs` (the page left last holds 256 MB: `critical` before, `moderate` now, and the release still drops that page) | iPhone 16, iOS 18 (2026-11-18, report of the verifier): spiegel.de alone took the area to 532 MB with its ads; the first release left it full, the second freed it to 354 MB in use, yet the demo said "Memory is almost full: this page uses most of it" with one tab and again on the next article. The second release exists to make room, so whether memory is short is known only after it. Local, Chromium at 393 x 852 with pixel ratio 3 and the shared profile: spiegel.de holds 290 to 300 MB of malloc after its load and 350 to 390 MB after scrolling the front page, the area 360 to 520 MB; with a budget of 300 MB the second release still leaves the area full there (315 MB of area, 243 MB in use), so that stays `critical`. Local A/B, scrolling the front page with a budget of 400 MB, the engines one after the other: before, `critical` at 451 MB of area with 291 MB still in use after the second release; after, `moderate` at 430 and 436 MB with 268 and 273 MB in use. Not taken: the bytes in use against the budget (see #497), a lower check step. Safari ending the page with five tabs is the host footprint outside the engine memory and stays open (`docs/status.md`) |
| 619 | A style sheet that a script inserts holds back neither the style of elements nor scripts while it loads, like Chrome and HTML | Patch 0160: `Style::Scope::addPendingSheet` takes whether the sheet blocks rendering; a sheet that does not (a `link` the parser did not create, a `style` whose `@import` a script made load, both without `blocking=render`) goes into a set of its own, `m_elementsWithNonBlockingPendingSheets`, which `hasPendingSheets`, `hasPendingSheetsBeforeBody` and `hasPendingSheetsInBody` leave out, and applies once it arrived (`didChangeActiveStyleSheetCandidates`, like an inactive sheet) instead of after every pending sheet. `HTMLLinkElement` decides with WebKit's own `isImplicitlyPotentiallyRenderBlocking` (created by the parser) and the `blocking` attribute, `InlineStyleSheetOwner` with `m_isParsingChildren` and the attribute. Test: `tests/core/script-stylesheet-test.mjs` (a page of 2000 elements inserts a sheet the server holds: an element added afterwards stayed unrendered, a layout read after a rendering update with a new element took a median of 14 ms instead of 0 ms (a restyle of the whole document), a script of the parser after the one that inserted a sheet never ran while it loaded; all pass now, also in Firefox) | Finding of 2026-11-18 (duolingo.com, coursera.org and cash.ch, single steps of the engine thread of 6.5 to 8.8 s while they load, Chromium at most 0.6 s). duolingo.com inserts Google's sign-in sheet (`accounts.google.com/gsi/style`), which took 8 to 11 s here, its body arriving only after a long script of the page's reCAPTCHA frame, and reads the height of its cookie banner every 100 ms for 2 s: WebKit styled no new element meanwhile and each read restyled the whole document from scratch (`Document::updateLayout` with pending sheets and elements left without style schedules a full rebuild, 4557 elements in 150 to 210 ms), up to 20 times in a row. Local A/B with a build that logs every style resolution of 25 ms or more (3 interleaved runs of 45 s, Chromium host): 8 to 17 full restyles of 100 ms or more of duolingo's document before, 1 after; style resolutions of 25 ms or more 2.5 to 4.3 s before, 1.5 to 1.6 s after. coursera.org showed only its header and footer until 8 s in one of three loads before (a script-inserted sheet held back its main content), at 3.4 s with the patch. | 620 | Form controls without author styles have Chrome's metrics and border contrast | Patch 0161. `SystemFontDatabaseWasm.cpp` gives the shorthands `-webkit-small-control`, `-webkit-mini-control` and `-webkit-control` (the font `html.css` gives inputs, selects and buttons) 2pt less than the default 16px, 13.333px, like Chrome's `LayoutThemeFontProvider` and Firefox; the other system fonts stay 16px. `themeAdwaita.css` gets a block for `WTF_PLATFORM_WASM`: buttons 1 px above and below their text and Chrome's margins of checkboxes (3px 3px 3px 4px) and radios (3px 3px 0 5px); `RenderThemeAdwaita` makes checkboxes and radios 13 px instead of 12 and keeps 1 px (`menuListButtonPaddingBlock`) instead of 5 above and below the text of a select, also of one the page styled. `Adwaita.h` draws the borders of text fields, buttons, selects, checkboxes and radios in Chrome's #767676 (hovered toggles #4f4f4f; dark #858585 and #acacac) instead of black or white at alpha 50. With the same font (Noto Sans, Chrome too) the heights are now Chrome's: 24, 24 and 22 px. The metrics go into the theme's own sheet and code of the port instead of `html.css`, which keeps WebKit's macOS values for the other ports. Not taken: Arial-like metrics (the engine ships only Noto Sans, whose taller line makes every control 3 px taller than Chrome's with Arial), Chrome's look of the controls (Adwaita stays, #21) |
| 621 | The menu key and Shift+F10 open the context menu like Chrome on Linux | Engine (`WebView::keyEvent`): a keydown of the menu key without modifiers or of Shift+F10 (Ctrl, Alt and Meta make it another shortcut) that the page did not handle calls `contextMenuForKey`, like WebKit2's `WebPage::contextMenuForKeyEvent` and Chrome's `WebFrameWidgetImpl`: `EventHandler::sendContextMenuEventForKey` in the focused frame, at the lower left corner of the focused element or the end of the selection, then the host's `context-menu` at the event's point through `showContextMenu`, which the right click shares; the key counts as handled, also when the page cancelled the event. Patch 0162: without a focused element or selection the point is the visible corner of the view, not the document's corner, which lay above the view on a scrolled page. Core (`keyboard.ts`): a `contextmenu` event of the hidden input field comes from the keyboard (it takes no pointer); Firefox on Linux sends it instead of a keydown, so the core cancels it and presses the menu key in the page, unless the last keydown was a menu key the page got already (Firefox on Windows sends it after the key). Every key sent resets `menuByTouch`, so a menu from a key is never the short menu of a long press. Keydown on every host, also on Windows, where Chrome takes the menu key's keyup | Keyboard users reach Copy, Open link in new tab and the rest of the menu, and pages get the `contextmenu` event of the key like in Chrome; no new export, the engine answers the key it already gets |
| 622 | The context menu of a video or audio has Chrome's media items | Engine (`engine/src/media/MediaMenu.cpp`, no patch): the event `context-menu` gets `media`, null or `{type, url, canSave, loop, canLoop, controls, canToggleControls, pictureInPicture}` for the `<video>` or `<audio>` of the menu's hit test, which counts the controls (UA shadow content) as their element like Chrome's and WebKit's menus. Chrome's rules: `canLoop` false for a `srcObject` stream or after an error, `canToggleControls` only for a video with a picture that is not fullscreen and has no error (an `<audio>` without controls would disappear for good), `canSave` from `MediaPlayerPrivateWasm::canSaveMediaData` (WebKit's own hook: a file at an http(s) address, not MSE or a stream, like WebKit's GStreamer player), `url` the `currentSrc` (a `blob:` address for MSE, empty for a stream). New export `bl_media_action(id, x, y, action, enable)` (abiVersion 44, `WebView::mediaActionAt`) hit tests the point again like Chrome's `MediaPlayerActionAt` (`WebView::menuHitTest`, now shared with Copy image) and in a user gesture sets `loop` or `controls`, or enters or leaves picture-in-picture; `enable` instead of a toggle like Chrome's `MediaPlayerAction`, so a state that changed after the menu opened ends as the item said. Picture-in-picture stays in the polyfill (`WebRTCPolyfill.cpp`, #119): it leaves one function on its bridge (`bridge.pictureInPictureMenu`; `installPageExtensions` now keeps each window's bridge under a private name, `pageBridge`, for what polyfills leave for the engine) that answers `unavailable`, `available` or `active` (its own window element, metadata, `disablepictureinpicture`, error, a host player or a live track) and enters or leaves with its own `requestPictureInPicture`, which pages cannot replace; without the function (no `pip` feature, Firefox without the API) the state is `unsupported`. Core: `ContextMenuInfo.media` (`ContextMenuMedia`, `menu-media.ts`), `View.mediaAction(x, y, action, enable)`, type `MediaAction`. UI: `defaultMenuItems` adds Chrome's order after the image items (`RenderViewContextMenu::AppendVideoItems`): `loop`, `show-controls`, `open-media`, `save-media`, `copy-media-address`, `picture-in-picture` (only when supported), audio texts for audio, the media address as the header after a long press; `MenuAction.checked` renders `role=menuitemcheckbox` with `aria-checked`, the demo draws a check mark in a gutter all items of such a menu share; texts `loop`, `showControls`, `pictureInPicture`, `openVideoInNewTab`, `saveVideoAs`, `copyVideoAddress` and the audio ones. Tests: `tests/demo/media-menu-test.mjs` (Chromium and Firefox: the items in order with their states, Loop and Show controls change the attributes, Copy video address, Save video as with the file's bytes, Open video in new tab, Picture in picture in and out with the page's events past a replaced `requestPictureInPicture`, the audio items), `packages/ui/test/menu.test.mjs` | Finding: a right click on a video showed only the page items, while Chrome offers Loop, Show controls, Picture in picture and open, save and copy the address. WebKit's `HitTestResult` already knows the media element, the engine just never reported it. Hit testing again instead of keeping the element follows Chrome and Copy image (#511): no state in the engine between menu and click. Picture-in-picture through the polyfill's own function instead of calling `requestPictureInPicture` on the element: Chrome's menu works also where the page replaced it, and the polyfill owns the window state the page sees (`pictureInPictureElement`, the events); a second copy of that state in C++ would drift. Leaving the function on the bridge instead of handing it to a native call keeps the start of every window as it was: a first version registered it through the media bridge, whose native function the start had never run, so it sat in a deferred chunk and `deferred-test` saw the start fetch chunks. Not done: Copy video frame (Chrome copies the frame at its natural size; the engine has the host video's frames only while copying them, at the size on screen, so it belongs to the core with the host `<video>`, open in `docs/status.md`) |
| 623 | The pages' `prefers-reduced-motion` and `prefers-contrast` follow the host | The core watches the host's `matchMedia` for `(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`, `(prefers-contrast: more)` and `(prefers-contrast: less)` next to the color scheme (`packages/core/src/screen.ts`) and sends them as flags 8, 16 and 32 of `bl_set_screen` (abiVersion 45). `WasmPlatform::ScreenInfo` keeps them; the Adwaita theme answers WebCore's `userPrefersReducedMotion` and `userPreferredContrast` from it on the port (patch 0163; GTK and WPE read GTK's settings there), so WebCore's media query features stay unchanged and pages still see `forcedPrefersReducedMotionAccessibilityValue` overrides first. | 624 | Text fields are spell checked like Chrome on Linux: Hunspell in the engine | Engine (`engine/src/Spelling.cpp`, `SpellingMenu.cpp`, no WebKit patch): `EditorClient` turns continuous spell checking on while a dictionary for the first of the pages' languages exists and the preference allows it, and its `TextCheckerClient` answers with Hunspell 1.7.2 (`deps/recipes/hunspell.sh`, compiled from its sources; the release's configure does not know Emscripten), the library of Chrome's renderer on Linux, with en_US of SCOWL 2020.12.07 as LibreOffice ships it (`deps/recipes/dictionaries.sh`: every `dict-*` line of `sources.txt`, UTF-8 `<language>.aff` and `.dic` to `lazy/dictionaries`, index `lazy-dictionaries.txt` in the data package, mounted with `LazyFiles` at `/lazy/dictionaries`). The dictionary is chosen like Chrome's default, the browser language: the first language, exact (en-US: en_US) or the same language (en-GB: en_US), none for others, so a German user does not get every word underlined by an English dictionary. It loads on a thread of its own the first time a field needs it, started with `pthread_create` because `WTF::Thread::create` waits for the thread to run (a new web worker takes 60 to 180 ms); a load that failed is tried again after 30 s, words count as correct meanwhile, and its arrival checks the focused field. Chrome's word rules (`SpellCheckWord`, `SpellcheckCharAttribute`): ICU letter words, none with digits, none with letters of another script than the language's, none over 64 characters; password fields are not checked. `shouldEraseMarkersAfterChangeSelection` false: the underline stays while the caret is in the word, editing the word removes it until it is checked again, like Chrome. Context menu: a right click on an underlined word selects it before the page's `contextmenu` event (Blink's `SelectClosestMisspellingFromHitTestResult`); the event `context-menu` gets `spelling` (`{word, suggestions}`, at most 3 like Chrome's menu, from WebCore's `misspelledWordAtCaretOrRange`, so also the word with the caret after the menu key); new export `bl_spelling_action(id, action, word, replacement)` (abiVersion 46) replaces the word if it is still the misspelled one there (`replaceSelectionWithText` with `EditAction::InsertReplacement`, the input type `insertReplacementText` Chrome uses) or adds it to the dictionary, kept in `/data/spelling/custom-dictionary.txt` (at most 99 bytes a word like Chrome) for every view and the next start. Preference `spellcheck` (flag 16 of `bl_set_preferences`, default on) like Chrome's "Check for spelling errors": off removes the underlines at once. Core: `Preferences.spellcheck`, `ContextMenuInfo.spelling` (`ContextMenuSpelling`, `menu-spelling.ts`), `View.replaceMisspelling(word, replacement)`, `View.addToDictionary(word)`. UI: `defaultMenuItems` puts the corrections (`spelling-suggestion-<n>`) and Add to dictionary (text `addToDictionary`) first in an editable field, like `SpellingMenuObserver`. Demo: setting Spell check. Measured locally: the first underline about 0.5 s after the first focus (550 KB dictionary over a local server, parsed on its thread); the engine thread's longest pause after a focus 26 to 44 ms with spell checking and 24 to 36 ms without; after the focus of a field with 100 KB of text 49 ms in pieces of 5 to 13 ms, 261 ms as one piece; the loaded dictionary takes about 3 MB of the engine's memory. Not done: more dictionaries (adding one is a `dict-*` line pair, the licenses of most other languages' Hunspell dictionaries are GPL or LGPL and need a look), Chrome's Spell check submenu (languages, Ask Google), grammar; Hunspell's code (about 250 KB) stays in `engine.wasm` until `engine/hot-functions.txt` is renewed | Finding: text fields had no spell checking at all: `EditorClient` returned false for continuous spell checking and its `TextCheckerClient` was empty, so no misspelled word was underlined and the context menu had no corrections, while Chrome and Safari check every field. No web API exposes the host browser's spell checker, so the engine needs its own; Hunspell is what Chrome uses on Linux and WebKitGTK through Enchant, with free dictionaries for many languages, and a lazy file keeps its dictionary off the start. Answering WebCore's requests at once keeps all marking and removing of underlines in WebCore; the asynchronous `SpellChecker` already removes the old underlines of what it checks. `tests/demo/spelling-test.mjs`: 8 of 15 checks failed on the engine before (no underline, no menu items), all 15 pass now in Chromium and Firefox, also the added word after a restart; `packages/ui/test/menu.test.mjs`, `deps/tests/smoke.cpp` (Hunspell with a small dictionary) |

On release the engine sends the final edges and then `context-menu` for the selection (no link, image or media, no `contextmenu` event for the page), like Chrome's menu after a handle drag. Core (`selection-handles.ts`): two drops of 22 px in touch areas of 44 px in a layer over the canvas and its damage tiles, at the bottoms of the caret lines (`--bl-selection-handle-color`, default `#1a73e8`); a drag captures the pointer, keeps the finger's distance to the middle of the line and sends the newest point once per display frame. `ContextMenuInfo.selectionBounds` (the selection with its handles, for a long press and a released handle) lets `showContextMenu` open the menu above the selection, else below it, so the handles stay reachable | Finding: on a Pixel 9 and an iPhone 16 a long press selected a word without any handles, so a phone could select more than one word only with Select all. WebKit draws no touch selection handles itself (on iOS they come from the UI process), so the embedder has to provide them; drawing them in the host keeps them sharp and responsive during a busy engine, while the engine owns the positions and the moves, where layout and editing boundaries are known. `tests/core/selection-handles-test.mjs`: 11 of 18 checks failed before (no handles, the drags changed nothing), all 18 pass now in Chromium and Firefox; `packages/ui/test/browser.mjs` checks the menu above and below a selection; checked on an iPhone 16 with Safari 18 (handles under the word, the end handle dragged over four words, the menu again with the new selection) |
| 626 | Tail calls of compiled code replace the caller's frame like the interpreter's; a bound function's frame is not the incumbent (changes #326, #456) | `engine/jit/WasmJIT.cpp`: at `op_tail_call` and `op_tail_call_varargs` a compiled function returns status -1 with the call's bytecode offset in its frame and, for `op_tail_call`, the callee, argv and argc in `pendingTailCall`. The interpreter, when it ran the function (`wasmJITEnter`, `wasmJITLoop`), goes on at that instruction and makes its own tail call. The module's call function of a compiled caller moves `this` and the arguments to where the frame ended (the interpreter's `prepareForTailCall`) and calls a compiled callee of the table there, again for the next tail calls, or a host function (`hostTailCallFromCompiledCode`); everything else, and the C++ paths of calls (`callInFrame`, `callFromCompiledCode`), go through `finishTailCall`, which sets up a varargs frame and checks the callee while the frame still stands, then moves it and calls from the frame's caller, a level of nesting like an ordinary call. The result goes into the `this` slot of the first frame. Patch 0164: WebCore's `callerGlobalObject` (the incumbent realm) skips the frames of bound functions, which have no execution context in ECMA-262. Test: `tests/engine/pages/tail-calls.html` with the JIT and without (postMessage through `call`, `apply`, `Reflect.apply` and bound functions of a frame and of a removed frame 6000 times each, `Error.stack`, results of tail calls with more and fewer arguments, spread, exceptions, a value that is no function, host functions, chains and a recursion of 200000) | Cloudflare's Turnstile gave no token with the JIT (0 in 13 runs, also on a Pixel 9): tail calls were ordinary calls in compiled code, so once JSC's `Function.prototype.call` builtin of another frame compiled (about 2000 calls) its frame stayed below `postMessage`, the incumbent realm was that frame's (removed: `source` null) and Turnstile ignored the `complete` message ("Ignored message from unexpected source"). The repro of the survey (`ncall.call(pm, parent, m)`, `nw.Reflect.apply`, `napply.call`, a removed frame's `call`, 6000 each): 1963, 4050, 0 and 1965 of 6000 messages with the right source before, 6000 now, like Chrome and Firefox; bound functions of another frame 0 of 6000 without the JIT before (the bound function's realm, Safari's behaviour), 6000 now like Chrome and Firefox. Turnstile (local, Chromium host): peet.ws managed, non-interactive and invisible give their token, scrapingcourse.com and nopecha.com go on after the click; on a Pixel 9 the managed token 4 s and scrapingcourse 7 s after the tap. Speed in the engine (1 million calls, median of 5): compiled to compiled tail call 59 to 69 ms against 58 to 105 before, to a host function 94 to 143 against 102 to 164, `f.call` 61 to 87 against 64 to 83; a strict recursion of 100 levels 83 to 131 against 749 to 856 ms (it nested). A compiled function with a tail call called from interpreted code costs about 120 ns more per call (340 to 410 against 230 to 240 ms): the interpreter makes the tail call and enters the callee. Not taken: a compiled frame that only hides from stack walks (the JS stack would still grow, and the interpreter's realm and `Error.stack` would differ from compiled code's), the whole tail call in the caller's WASM (the frame's `CodeBlock` would have to stay alive and its header be restored for the interpreter's `op_ret`) |
| 627 | A termination of the engine ends with the outermost entry into JS (adds to #501, #583) | Patch 0165 (`VM::executeEntryScopeServicesOnExit`, `Source/JavaScriptCore/runtime/VM.cpp`): for the VM the embedder names at the start (`bl_jsc_set_vm_terminating_single_entries`, the main thread's, `engine/src/EngineLoop.cpp`), the end of the outermost entry clears a TerminationException still pending, where JSC already clears the termination request. `runNestedWork` clears both after a work item of the nested event loop, whose entries are nested. Workers (their VM ends with a termination, `forbidExecutionOnTermination`) and the jsc shell keep JSC's behaviour. Test: `tests/engine/script-stop-test.mjs` (the user leaves a page while guest WebAssembly's import runs long from a promise reaction, then from a `FinalizationRegistry` callback: another view's chain of promise reactions goes on and a new view's `Promise.then` runs; before, the second round failed both in 3 of 3 runs) | The script watchdog and a navigation of the user terminate single entries of the VM all pages share (#501, #583). WebCore's microtask checkpoint and JSC's `DeferredWorkTimer` leave the TerminationException pending, for a worker's VM, which ends with it. Here the next checkpoints of other sites ran their first microtask against it and dropped their queues, and the `DeferredWorkTimer` (cleanups of a `FinalizationRegistry`) took it as the end of the VM and set `executionForbidden` for good: no promise reaction of any view ran again until a restart, while timers, synchronous script and rendering went on. Found on hCaptcha's demo, whose `hsw.js` runs its proof of work in guest WebAssembly from promise reactions and frees its objects from a `FinalizationRegistry` (wasm-bindgen); a debug build showed the pending exception after 2 to 4 checkpoints, then the `DeferredWorkTimer` with it, then `executionForbidden` set. Measured locally (Chromium host, the demo left for example.com 2.5, 3, 3.5, 4 and 4.5 s after its load, 3 runs each, then a probe on example.com and in a new tab): before, every probe after the first or second stop missed the promise reaction, `queueMicrotask`, `WebAssembly.instantiate` and `compile`, `crypto.subtle.digest` and `fetch`; after, all six in both places in 15 of 15 runs (9 with a stop, in `hsw.js` and at lines 116 and 187 of the WebAssembly bridge's script), and the next demo loads drew the checkbox after 3.2 to 4.0 s (5 of 5) in the same engine. Not taken: clearing in each place that leaves it (WebCore's checkpoint, the `DeferredWorkTimer`, promise jobs from C++); the end of the outermost entry covers them all, the queue of the stopped checkpoint is still dropped as JSC does |
| 628 | While the user keeps working, a paused cross-site frame runs a slice of 100 ms after every 500 ms paused (changes #589) | `engine/src/FramePause.cpp`: the pause ends as before when no urgent work waits and input waits for the frame or none came for 1 s; while input keeps coming it now also ends once it has lasted 500 ms (`pauseWhileUserWorks`) and no urgent work waits. The script then runs a slice of 100 ms (`sliceTime`, the interval of the script watchdog) in which `pauseFrameScriptForUser` declines; `requestSafepointAfter` (`engine/src/EngineLoop.cpp`, a safepoint trap from JSC's VM traps queue) brings it to a safepoint at the slice's end, where input that came meanwhile pauses it again. The slice is wall time: ended by the watchdog's next look instead (its interval counts CPU time), keys of the test waited 253 ms, until the next key. Local A/B (2026-11-19, Chromium host, phone viewport, 1x, edition.cnn.com, a touch every 250 ms from the start of the load, 4 runs each, alternating): before the Stripe frame paused 77.9, 75.6 and 68.8 s (until the end of the run) and the load event did not come within 90 s in 3 of 4 runs (the fourth paused nothing, 18.4 s); now 18.4, 18.3, 19.8 and 19.7 s, the longest pause 683, 830, 729 and 924 ms; without touches 14.6 to 21.6 s. The touches' p90 in the first 18 s 706 to 885 ms against 765 to 1218 ms before, hovering moves (`input-latency.mjs`) p90 587 and 665 against 566 and 1489 ms. Hovering is not input that holds (#364), so Cloudflare's Turnstile (peet.ws managed) with the mouse moving every 50 ms showed its checkbox after 46 to 60 s as without (51 to 61 s), as before. Not taken: pausing only the script and letting the frame's loads go on (its documents would run script while the script waits in the middle), a share of every RunLoop step (the pause can only end between two work items of the nested loop), a slice per input (the page's rendering would wait for the frame again, #602) |
| 629 | Console calls run page code only for their first argument, like Safari (changes #274) | Patch 0056 (`FrameConsoleClient::messageWithTypeAndLevel`, `Source/WebCore/page/FrameConsoleClient.cpp`): the message for the embedder starts from `messageText`, WebKit's own conversion of the first argument, and formats the other arguments without page code (`argumentPreview`): primitives as text (a symbol as `Symbol(s)`), errors by `ErrorInstance::sanitizedToString` (name and message only from data properties), a proxy as `Proxy`, other objects by `JSObject::calculatedClassName` like the inspector's preview (`Object`, `Point`, `Array`, `HTMLHtmlElement`; it reads `constructor` and `Symbol.toStringTag` only as data properties). DataDome's slider on www.nytimes.com still blocks (status.md); its warning "Please close the DevTools panel" is printed always, not a verdict. | 630 | The JIT's fast paths of property, element, keyed and scope access are functions of the module; its warm-up counts by `UnlinkedCodeBlock` and ends sooner (changes #566) | `engine/jit/WasmJIT.cpp`: the fast paths of `get_by_id`, `get_by_val`, `put_by_val`, `resolve_scope`, `get_from_scope`, `put_to_scope` and the `Math.imul` check of a call are emitted once per module as functions (`emitFastPathFunction`, operands as tag and payload parameters, the destination as the register's address, the metadata as a parameter) that every site calls; slow paths go through one function of the module (`SlowPathCall`). A `get_from_scope` of a variable the bytecode generator resolved in a closure reads it inline without a slow path, like the interpreter. The warm-up counts fires by `UnlinkedCodeBlock` (entries not fired for 60 s dropped above 4096) and needs 64 fires plus one per 6 bytes, at most 512 (before one per 2 bytes, at most 2048). Test: `tests/jsc/jit-size.js` with `run.sh` (the module of a VM-like function of 74 KB of bytecode at most 12 times its bytecode: 31.1 times before, 8.3 now) | Cloudflare's Turnstile (peet.ws managed, survey item 8) took 57 to 151 s to its checkbox against 3 to 5 s natively, and `BL_JIT=0` was no slower. `perf stat` on the engine thread showed why: in the VM's compiled code (one function of 50 to 66 KB of bytecode, 1.6 MB of WASM, about 3 MB of machine code) 0.58 instructions per cycle against the interpreter's 1.22, 24 % of the instruction fetches missed the L1 cache and 23 % of the cycles waited for them; the profile had no hot spot, every instruction cost the same. 44 % of the VM's instructions were scope accesses (its strings and state in global variables) of about 300 bytes of WASM each, an element access about 850. The same dispatch with 256 cases ran 3 to 5 times slower than with 16 for the same work (jsc shell), now 1.2 to 1.9 times. A sampler at the watchdog's checks (not kept) showed 37 % of the challenge's JS interpreted: the VM's functions of 4 to 12 KB needed 1400 to 2048 fires, and every full GC threw their `CodeBlock` away (not compiled, older than 5 s) and with it the count. Result (5 interleaved runs each): the checkbox after 31.5 to 44.2 s (median 40.1) instead of 40.8 to 72.5 s (median 54.0), the longest step 9.7 to 17.3 s instead of 12.9 to 26.3 s; Firefox host 43 to 52 s instead of 44 to 68 s; 20min.ch compiles 241 functions into 1.4 MB in 50 s instead of 181 into 1.1 MB (#566 had 789 into 12.1 MB). The criterion (30 s, steps under 5 s) is not reached, status.md. Not taken: a JS task of the challenge cut into slices (it runs to completion, the frame already pauses for input, #589), V8 flags (not ours to set), compiling the large functions sooner by time since their first fire (fires already tell how long they ran), keeping `CodeBlock`s that warm up from the GC (a WebKit patch and more memory for a count) |
| 631 | A regular expression's cost is measured with a clock in microseconds; the first call decides and a pattern in the host is measured again only rarely (changes #348) | Patch 0166: WTF's `MonotonicTime::now()` on Emscripten reads `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, which is the host's `performance.now()` (5 us in Chromium, 20 us in Firefox), instead of the fallback for unknown systems, the wall clock through `Date.now()` in whole milliseconds and not monotonic. Patch 0073 (`Yarr::interpret`): the first call of a pattern is measured and decides (before the cheapest of the first 4); a pattern goes to the host's RegExp when it costs at least 10 ns per character (4 times the copy of the input into a JS string, 2.5 ns per character in Chromium, the call itself about 1.5 us) and the call at least 30 us (before 40 ns and 30 us). Calls that stay in the interpreter are measured again every 32nd call as before; calls that would go to the host only as call 32, 64, 128 and so on, and not at all while the pattern's last call in the host took 30 us more than its copy and call (the interpreter is 6 to 25 times slower than V8 on the same pattern). A measured call counts at most as much as the one before it, so a single call the system held up does not move a pattern. Tests: `tests/engine/wtf-tests.cpp` (`MonotonicTime` steps below 100 us and never back; failed before with steps of 1 ms), `tests/core/regexp-test.mjs` (a cookie regexp first called 4 times on a 200 character cookie and then on the 4 K cookie: 2 calls 30 ms before, as long as in the interpreter, now 0 to 1 ms); core and jsc suites pass, the engine suite except font-stacks as on the old engine | Every measured call of the interpreter read 0 or 1 ms: a pattern whose first calls took below 1 ms measured 0 ns per character and stayed in the interpreter until its 32nd call. On 20min.ch the cookie regexp `(^\|[^;]+)\s*20min-Session\s*=\s*([^;]+)` was first called on a short cookie early in the load and then cost 46 ms per call in the interpreter on the 2.5 K cookie (415 of the 619 ms of Yarr while an article opened); the patterns that did go to the host were measured in the interpreter again every 32nd call (`dataLayerLogger`, 47 ms in that window). Profiles of 20min.ch (phone emulation, unthrottled, local, 3 interleaved runs each): `Yarr::Interpreter::matchDisjunction` while an article opens 0.66 to 0.85 % of the engine thread instead of 7.3 to 11.6 % (58 to 87 ms instead of 780 to 1004 ms), on Back 0.35 to 0.59 % instead of 4.2 to 5.2 %; the host's RegExp 2.5 to 3.5 % instead of 2.1 to 2.4 %. How long the tap takes did not change measurably: the article in the DOM after 10.2 to 11.9 s instead of 9.5 to 12.4 s, at a fourth of the CPU (cgroup per thread, 3 interleaved runs) after 38.8 to 50.3 s instead of 38.6 to 49.8 s (medians 40.8 and 41.9 s), Back 32.0 to 44.5 s instead of 43.2 to 61.2 s; the runs vary by 25 % and the regexps were about 9 % of the engine thread's work in the tap. Microbenchmark in the engine (Chromium host): a stack trace regexp on 65 characters 2000 times 16 to 32 ms instead of 41 to 110 ms (V8 14 ms), `replace` loops over long strings and short patterns unchanged. The clock change does not change what pages see: WebCore rounds `performance.now()` to 1 ms like Safari. Not taken: deciding on the minimum of the first 2 calls (one more full interpretation of every expensive pattern, 35 ms for a 20min.ch cookie regexp), 10 us as the smallest call for the host (short patterns with one delayed measurement went to the host and a `replace` of 36 characters took 5 times as long), a clock of its own in Yarr (the wall clock in milliseconds is wrong for every `MonotonicTime` user) |

| 633 | Hosts with plenty of memory release caches from 512 MB of malloc area (changes #61) | `packages/core/src/config.ts` passes `BL_MEMORY_RELEASE_MB=512` when the host has 8 GB of memory or more: `navigator.deviceMemory` of 8 or more where the browser reports it (Chromium: a Moto G71 reports 4, desktop Chromium 8 to 32), else every host that is not a phone or tablet (Firefox and Safari report none; Android, iPhone and iPad keep 192). Measured in `docs/status.md` (heavy pages on phones). |
| 650 | An element whose `::before` or `::after` is animated, and the animated pseudo-element, keep their styles before animations as the base of animation-only updates (adds to #642) | Patch 0181: `TreeResolver::createAnimatedElementUpdate` keeps a copy of the resolved style as `lastStyleChangeEventStyle` also for an element without animations of its own when its `::before` or `::after` has keyframe effects, so `styleForStyleable` starts an animation-only update from it like for an animated element; `resolvePseudoElement` starts an animated `::before` or `::after` from its own `lastStyleChangeEventStyle` (WebKit kept it but matched all rules again) unless the cascade overrode an animated property (`!important`) or the element's update changed what it inherits (`animationLeavesPseudoElementBase`, the check of #642 now against the base). A `::before` or `::after` without animations keeps its style only if it has no base left, so one whose animation just ended is resolved again. Test: `tests/engine/pages/pseudo-animations.html` (same computed style as a fresh style through frames, an element animation of what the `::after` inherits, an `!important` rule, a class change and the end of the animation; a frame of 200 pulsing `::after` at most 30 % of restyling their elements: 3 against 28 ms, before 21 against 28 ms) | edition.cnn.com pulses the `::after` of 6 ad slots and every frame matched all rules of the slots and their `::after`: idle style was half of the engine thread. Measured (2026-11-23, local, Chromium host 1280x800, profile of the 20 s after the first 45 s, 2 interleaved runs, loaded machine): style 11.7 to 15.0 % of the engine thread's busy time instead of 50.1 to 50.3 %, `SelectorChecker::matchRecursively` none instead of 0.63 to 0.75 s per 10 s; renderer 2.69 to 2.84 cores against 2.83 to 2.96, plain Chromium 2.87 to 3.10 (CNN's playing videos took most of it that day). Before, since #642 a `::before` or `::after` whose animation was cancelled kept its last animated values until the next full style; fixed here |
| 651 | Phones keep the WASM memory near the malloc area, and a memory release waits until the user paused (changes #61, #128, #329) | `engine/js/engine-memory.js`: when the memory got its full 4 GB maximum and a resizable `ArrayBuffer` of 3 GB can be reserved (a 64 bit address space), it sets `BL_MEMORY_RESERVE=0`, and `MemoryWatch.cpp` grows no 128 MB reserve ahead of the malloc area there (a test limit still does). `engine.cmake`: `MEMORY_GROWTH_GEOMETRIC_CAP=16MB`. Patch 0183: `MarkedBlock::blockSize` 16 KB on WASM (slabs of 32 blocks, 512 KB), `updatePercentageOfPagedOutPages` off there (no `mincore`). Patch 0182: WTF threads that are not `JavaScript` or `Audio` get 1 MB stacks on WASM. `MemoryWatch.cpp`: a release due within 3 s of the last input to a visible view, navigation of the host or view shown (`sinceUserWorked`, `HiddenViews.cpp`) waits for a timer until 3 s passed without input, at most 10 s, not above the budget or near the limit. Tests: `tests/core/memory-release-test.mjs` (a release a press made due comes 3 s after it, without input at once), `tests/core/memory-test.mjs` (a 64 bit host: memory at most 96 MB above malloc in use with 488 MB in use; before 742 MB with 512 MB) | A Moto G71 reached 746 and 763 MB on 20min.ch and blick.ch (home, article, Back) and spent 318 to 590 ms in a release inside a tap or Back. About 150 MB of that memory were never touched: the reserve finds the limit of a 32 bit browser, where it is needed, but a 64 bit browser does not fail a growth before the device is out of memory, and Emscripten's growth by 20 % (up to 96 MB) stays for good as WASM memory never shrinks. 64 KB blocks (the WASM page as `CeilingOnPageSize`) kept 2 MB slabs for few live cells, and the 4 MB stacks of the 16 or so threads were almost unused (at most about 100 KB). Measured 2026-11-24 with the same flow: Moto G71 544 to 545 MB on 20min.ch and 466 MB on blick.ch against 643 and 637 MB with the old engine in the same session; locally at 4x throttling 586 to 589 against 765 MB and 517 to 535 against 760 to 788 MB; no release over 200 ms within 3 s of a tap or Back. Releasing earlier (a lower threshold) would only add more blocking releases; a release in a tap is pointless as the page's answer to it takes the memory again |
| 652 | Work on the engine thread runs in the order it was queued: a timer that came due during a long task runs after the messages posted before its deadline, like in Chrome and the HTML standard | Patch 0184: a function of WTF's RunLoop carries its queue time, the time it was dispatched, or for a function that another function of the same thread dispatches (the next step of delivering a message, such as the port message of a worker) that function's time, for one step only, so a function that keeps dispatching itself gets newer times; a RunLoop cycle runs the functions waiting at its start, and the steps they dispatched with their time, before the timers that were due then (`RunLoopGeneric.cpp`, before: the timers first). A task of WebCore's event loop carries its queue time too (now, or the time of the RunLoop function that delivers it while no script runs; what a script queues, also one that a function runs such as a script of the parser, is queued now), and the page's event loop is due when its oldest waiting task was queued (`WindowEventLoop::scheduleToRun`, before: always now), also for the tasks a pass leaves for the next one, so WebCore's timer heap orders its tasks against the timers by that time. A message between two ports of the same page is delivered by the task queued when it was posted instead of a second task. A pass of WebCore's timers fires no timer that was scheduled during it for a time already past (`ThreadTimers.cpp`): a pass of the event loop that stopped for input keeps its oldest task's time and is due again at once, and runs in the next pass, after the input. Only `.cpp` files: `RunLoop.h` and `EventLoop.h` are in the precompiled prefix headers, a change there rebuilds all of WebKit (about 30 CPU hours). Test: `tests/engine/pages/task-order.html` (5 of 7 orders wrong before: a window message behind other tasks of the event loop, a MessagePort of the page, a worker, a worker's MessagePort and a frame's MessagePort; all 7 right now, as in Chromium; Firefox has 3 of them wrong) | Finding of 2026-11-24 (reCAPTCHA v2 on a cold engine start never recovered after "reCAPTCHA Timeout"): the demo page waits 15 s for the answers of its anchor frame on a MessagePort, the anchor 5 s for its worker. A port message took a RunLoop function and then a task of the event loop queued only when that function ran, and a cycle fired the due timers first, so a timer that came due during the anchor's long task ran before an answer posted before its deadline. Measured (local, Chromium host, a new browser and engine per run, interleaved): at a load of 8 to 33 the invisible demo gives the token or the challenge after one click in every run (26 with the patch, 26 without; 6 pairs at a load of 12 to 16: 3.3 to 7.0 s after the click, median 4.0, against 3.4 to 4.7 s, median 4.0); at a load of 35 to 50 in 1 of 8 with it (the one at 18) and 0 of 4 without: there the worker answers after the anchor's 5 s (its script and `importScripts` load through the busy engine thread, then 860 KB run in the interpreter), the anchor computes on the engine thread in one step of 9 to 14 s and the page's 15 s pass before the answer is posted, so the order cannot help (docs/status.md). The checkbox in phone emulation (390x780, touch, a fourth of the CPU, load 9 to 19) opens the challenge 4.5 to 11.7 s after the tap (median 5.9, 13 of 14 within 10 s) against 1.8 to 6.6 s (median 6.2, 11 of 11); the slow run waited 4.7 s for the worker, before up to 3.1 s. The v3 score demo: 9.7 to 13.4 s (median 12.7) against 9.9 to 13.3 s (median 11.9), 6 pairs. A first version that stopped WebCore's timers after each one while input or a frame answer waited was not kept. The engine suite and 17 tests of the core suite (input, frames-busy, scroll-busy, frame-pause, worker-load, background, serviceworker and others, 10 of them again with the final version) pass. Not taken: Chrome's order also for what a task posts after a timer's deadline (Chrome queues the timer only when the task ended, after all it posted): that needs the start of the running task on every path that queues work; the order by time, as in the HTML standard, covers the messages posted before the deadline |
| 653 | Reading `navigator.onLine` fires no event; only a real change of the host does (adds to #158) | Patch 0008 (`platform/wasm/NetworkStateNotifierWasm.cpp`): `NetworkStateNotifier::startObserving`, run when the first listener comes (the first `Page`, `Worker` or the error page's reconnect), takes the host's state with `updateStateWithoutNotifying()`. | 654 | A large function of the JIT also compiles by its sampled time in the interpreter, not only by its fires (adds to #566, #630) | `engine/jit/WasmJIT.cpp`: a function whose warm-up is at its maximum of 512 fires (from about 2.7 KB of bytecode) also compiles once its time in the interpreter is 30 times the estimated cost of its compile (9 us per byte of bytecode: Liftoff's 30 MB/s on a desktop and a module of about 8 times the bytecode; at least 100 ms), by `UnlinkedCodeBlock` like the fires. The time is sampled: at a fire of such a function the JIT asks the embedder for a safepoint of the running script 5 ms later (`bl_jsc_set_jit_sample_requester`; `engine/src/EngineLoop.cpp` uses `requestSafepointAfter`, the `NeedSafepointGC` trap of the hidden views and frame pauses, only for its work VM), and at that safepoint (`bl_jsc_jit_sample` from `atSafepoint`) credits the time since the request (at most 20 ms) to the interpreted function on top, or to its caller when the safepoint is the entry of a function (bytecode offset 0), which ran nothing yet; while the sample lands in such a function the next one is asked for. A safepoint for other work sooner than 2.5 ms is no sample, a request whose safepoint never came is renewed after 50 ms. The `jsc` shell has no embedder and counts only fires. The JIT's log (`BL_JIT=2`) names the fires and sampled ms of each compile. Test: `tests/engine/jit-warmup-test.mjs` (a function of 10.5 KB whose loop runs 10000 long iterations, 20 fires: interpreted to the end before, compiled after 104 to 119 ms now; the cold functions stay interpreted) | Turnstile's second phase (from `pat` to `ci`, which Cloudflare also limits: `ci` sent 66 s or less after `fo` got 200, 76 s or more 400) had not been profiled: the interpreter was 35 to 55 % of the engine thread there, all in functions that compiled code called through the VM entry of the generic call, and the VM's large functions (19 to 78 KB) fired every 100 to 150 ms, so their 512 fires took 35 to 45 s (one step of 38 s). Measured ([jit.md](jit.md#measured)): with every renderer thread at a third of a CPU (the Moto G71's order) `ci` 28.9 s after `fo` (median of 5, 4 with 200) against 54.4 s (3 with 200); on a desktop host `pat` median 7.3 against 8.5 s and the checkbox 24.7 against 26.6 s after `rch` (10 runs each, within the spread but for a heavy VM, 36.7 against 59.3 s); Speedometer median 1.11 against 1.05. Not taken: crediting the top frame at a function's entry (a VM's handlers took its dispatch loop's samples, the large functions had 20 to 41 ms by their 512th fire), sampling functions from 380 bytes with a budget of at least 50 ms (checkbox median 23.7 against 27.2 s, but Speedometer below 1.0 in 6 of 15 runs against 0 of 14), 64 fires for functions from 8 KB (within the spread), a clock read at every fire (#632) |

Measured locally (2026-11-25, Chromium host with SwiftShader, 1280x800, 30 or 60 s after a 60 s settle, 5 runs against 5 interleaved): 1.3 to 6.7 resizes per 10 s, 14.7 in a run with 22 whole frames, which still take the view's size and back (two resizes each, as before); `bl_gpu_prepare` 0.01 instead of 6.2 s per 30 s, the compositor thread busy 5.8 instead of 11.9 s.
